USER     color_map = {0: '⬛', 1: '🟦', 2: '🟥', 3: '🟩', 4: '🟨', 5: '⬜'}
USER     return '\n'.join(''.join(color_map.get(cell, '❓') for cell in row) for row in grid.values)

USER ```
USER from rob_agi.colored_grid import ColoredGrid
USER from typing import List, Tuple, Set
USER import logging
USER import copy
USER 
USER logging.basicConfig(level=logging.DEBUG)
USER logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
USER 
USER def solve_776ffc46(input_grid: ColoredGrid) -> ColoredGrid:
USER     """
USER     Transforms the input grid by changing blue plus shapes to a target color.
USER     The target color (red or green) is determined by the color most frequently
USER     adjacent to gray borders, including diagonally adjacent cells. Only valid
USER     blue plus shapes (5 pixels in a + configuration) are transformed. Other blue
USER     shapes and colors remain unchanged. All transformations are applied simultaneously.
USER 
USER     1. Identify all gray borders in the grid.
USER     2. Count red and green pixels adjacent (including diagonally) to all gray borders collectively.
USER     3. Determine the target color based on the counts (red if tied).
USER     4. Find all blue plus shapes in the grid.
USER     5. Transform these blue plus shapes to the target color.
USER     6. Return the modified grid.
USER 
USER     If no red or green pixels are found adjacent to gray borders, return the original grid.
USER     """
USER     GRAY, BLUE, RED, GREEN = 5, 1, 2, 3
USER     rows, cols = len(input_grid.values), len(input_grid.values[0])
USER 
USER     def is_valid_cell(r: int, c: int) -> bool:
USER         return 0 <= r < rows and 0 <= c < cols
USER 
USER     def get_adjacent_cells(r: int, c: int) -> Set[Tuple[int, int]]:
USER         return {(r+dr, c+dc) for dr in [-1, 0, 1] for dc in [-1, 0, 1] 
USER                 if (dr != 0 or dc != 0) and is_valid_cell(r+dr, c+dc)}
USER 
USER     def is_blue_plus(r: int, c: int) -> bool:
USER         if input_grid.values[r][c] != BLUE:
USER             return False
USER         return all(is_valid_cell(r+dr, c+dc) and input_grid.values[r+dr][c+dc] == BLUE 
USER                    for dr, dc in [(0, 1), (1, 0), (0, -1), (-1, 0)])
USER 
USER     # Step 1 & 2: Identify gray borders and count adjacent colors
USER     border_cells = set()
USER     adjacent_cells = set()
USER     for r in range(rows):
USER         for c in range(cols):
USER             if input_grid.values[r][c] == GRAY:
USER                 border_cells.add((r, c))
USER                 adjacent_cells.update(get_adjacent_cells(r, c))
USER 
USER     adjacent_cells -= border_cells  # Remove gray cells from adjacent cells
USER 
USER     red_count = sum(1 for r, c in adjacent_cells if input_grid.values[r][c] == RED)
USER     green_count = sum(1 for r, c in adjacent_cells if input_grid.values[r][c] == GREEN)
USER 
USER     logger.debug(f"Gray border cells: {border_cells}")
USER     logger.debug(f"Adjacent cells: {adjacent_cells}")
USER     logger.debug(f"Red count: {red_count}, Green count: {green_count}")
USER 
USER     # Step 3: Determine the target color
USER     if red_count == 0 and green_count == 0:
USER         logger.info("No red or green cells adjacent to gray borders. Returning original grid.")
USER         return input_grid
USER 
USER     target_color = RED if red_count >= green_count else GREEN
USER     logger.info(f"Target color: {target_color}")
USER 
USER     # Step 4 & 5: Find and transform blue plus shapes
USER     result_grid = copy.deepcopy(input_grid.values)
USER     for r in range(rows):
USER         for c in range(cols):
USER             if is_blue_plus(r, c):
USER                 for dr, dc in [(0, 0), (0, 1), (1, 0), (0, -1), (-1, 0)]:
USER                     result_grid[r+dr][c+dc] = target_color
USER                 logger.debug(f"Transformed blue plus at ({r}, {c}) to color {target_color}")
USER 
USER     # Step 6: Return the modified grid
USER     return ColoredGrid(values=result_grid)
USER ```

USER Challenge 776ffc46: Transform blue plus shapes in a grid
USER 
USER Key observations:
USER 1. Only blue plus shapes (5 pixels in a + configuration) are transformed.
USER 2. The target color for transformation is determined by the color most frequently adjacent to gray borders.
USER 3. Gray (5) acts as a border and is not part of any region to be transformed.
USER 4. All transformations are applied simultaneously.
USER 5. Diagonal adjacency to gray borders is considered when counting red and green cells.
USER 
USER Rules:
USER 1. Find the target color by counting occurrences of red (2) and green (3) adjacent to all gray borders collectively.
USER 2. Choose the color with the higher count as the target color.
USER 3. If counts are equal, use red as the target color.
USER 4. Identify all blue plus shapes in the grid.
USER 5. Transform these blue plus shapes to the target color.
USER 6. Other blue shapes and colors remain unchanged.
USER 7. If no red or green cells are adjacent to gray borders, return the original grid unchanged.
USER 
USER Current issues:
USER 1. The function is correctly identifying red and green cells adjacent to gray borders.
USER 2. The target color determination is working correctly.
USER 3. The transformation of blue plus shapes is not being applied.
USER 
USER Lessons learned:
USER 1. The target color determination is working as expected.
USER 2. Counting occurrences of red and green, including diagonally adjacent cells, is correct.
USER 3. The issue lies in the application of the transformation to blue plus shapes.
USER 4. The current implementation is correctly handling the case where no red or green cells are adjacent to gray borders.
USER 
USER Next steps:
USER 1. Review and fix the logic for transforming blue plus shapes.
USER 2. Add more detailed debug logging to track the transformation process.
USER 3. Implement additional test cases to verify the transformation of blue plus shapes.
USER 4. Ensure that the transformation is applied to all valid blue plus shapes in the grid.

USER - Use sorting to order elements or colors based on specific criteria.
USER - Expand or contract grid regions based on rules.

USER - Implement pattern matching algorithms for repetitive structures.

USER rob_agi/attempts/c_776ffc46/main.py
USER ```
USER from rob_agi.colored_grid import ColoredGrid
USER from typing import List, Tuple, Set
USER import logging
USER import copy
USER 
USER logging.basicConfig(level=logging.DEBUG)
USER logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
USER 
USER def solve_776ffc46(input_grid: ColoredGrid) -> ColoredGrid:
USER     """
USER     Transforms the input grid by changing blue plus shapes to a target color.
USER     The target color (red or green) is determined by the color most frequently
USER     adjacent to gray borders, including diagonally adjacent cells. Only valid
USER     blue plus shapes (5 pixels in a + configuration) are transformed. Other blue
USER     shapes and colors remain unchanged. All transformations are applied simultaneously.
USER 
USER     1. Identify all gray borders in the grid.
USER     2. Count red and green pixels adjacent (including diagonally) to all gray borders collectively.
USER     3. Determine the target color based on the counts (red if tied).
USER     4. Find all blue plus shapes in the grid.
USER     5. Transform these blue plus shapes to the target color.
USER     6. Return the modified grid.
USER 
USER     If no red or green pixels are found adjacent to gray borders, return the original grid.
USER     """
USER     GRAY, BLUE, RED, GREEN = 5, 1, 2, 3
USER     rows, cols = len(input_grid.values), len(input_grid.values[0])
USER 
USER     def is_valid_cell(r: int, c: int) -> bool:
USER         return 0 <= r < rows and 0 <= c < cols
USER 
USER     def get_adjacent_cells(r: int, c: int) -> Set[Tuple[int, int]]:
USER         return {(r+dr, c+dc) for dr in [-1, 0, 1] for dc in [-1, 0, 1] 
USER                 if (dr != 0 or dc != 0) and is_valid_cell(r+dr, c+dc)}
USER 
USER     def is_blue_plus(r: int, c: int) -> bool:
USER         if input_grid.values[r][c] != BLUE:
USER             return False
USER         return all(is_valid_cell(r+dr, c+dc) and input_grid.values[r+dr][c+dc] == BLUE 
USER                    for dr, dc in [(0, 1), (1, 0), (0, -1), (-1, 0)])
USER 
USER     # Step 1 & 2: Identify gray borders and count adjacent colors
USER     border_cells = set()
USER     adjacent_cells = set()
USER     for r in range(rows):
USER         for c in range(cols):
USER             if input_grid.values[r][c] == GRAY:
USER                 border_cells.add((r, c))
USER                 adjacent_cells.update(get_adjacent_cells(r, c))
USER 
USER     adjacent_cells -= border_cells  # Remove gray cells from adjacent cells
USER 
USER     red_count = sum(1 for r, c in adjacent_cells if input_grid.values[r][c] == RED)
USER     green_count = sum(1 for r, c in adjacent_cells if input_grid.values[r][c] == GREEN)
USER 
USER     logger.debug(f"Gray border cells: {border_cells}")
USER     logger.debug(f"Adjacent cells: {adjacent_cells}")
USER     logger.debug(f"Red count: {red_count}, Green count: {green_count}")
USER 
USER     # Step 3: Determine the target color
USER     if red_count == 0 and green_count == 0:
USER         logger.info("No red or green cells adjacent to gray borders. Returning original grid.")
USER         return input_grid
USER 
USER     target_color = RED if red_count >= green_count else GREEN
USER     logger.info(f"Target color: {target_color}")
USER 
USER     # Step 4 & 5: Find and transform blue plus shapes
USER     result_grid = copy.deepcopy(input_grid.values)
USER     for r in range(rows):
USER         for c in range(cols):
USER             if is_blue_plus(r, c):
USER                 for dr, dc in [(0, 0), (0, 1), (1, 0), (0, -1), (-1, 0)]:
USER                     result_grid[r+dr][c+dc] = target_color
USER                 logger.debug(f"Transformed blue plus at ({r}, {c}) to color {target_color}")
USER 
USER     # Step 6: Return the modified grid
USER     return ColoredGrid(values=result_grid)

USER Challenge 776ffc46: Transform blue plus shapes in a grid
USER 
USER Key observations:
USER 1. Only blue plus shapes (5 pixels in a + configuration) are transformed.
USER 2. The target color for transformation is determined by the color most frequently adjacent to gray borders.
USER 3. Gray (5) acts as a border and is not part of any region to be transformed.
USER 4. All transformations are applied simultaneously.
USER 5. Diagonal adjacency to gray borders is considered when counting red and green cells.
USER 
USER Rules:
USER 1. Find the target color by counting occurrences of red (2) and green (3) adjacent to all gray borders collectively.
USER 2. Choose the color with the higher count as the target color.
USER 3. If counts are equal, use red as the target color.
USER 4. Identify all blue plus shapes in the grid.
USER 5. Transform these blue plus shapes to the target color.
USER 6. Other blue shapes and colors remain unchanged.
USER 7. If no red or green cells are adjacent to gray borders, return the original grid unchanged.
USER 
USER Current issues:
USER 1. The function is correctly identifying red and green cells adjacent to gray borders.
USER 2. The target color determination is working correctly.
USER 3. The transformation of blue plus shapes is not being applied.
USER 
USER Lessons learned:
USER 1. The target color determination is working as expected.
USER 2. Counting occurrences of red and green, including diagonally adjacent cells, is correct.
USER 3. The issue lies in the application of the transformation to blue plus shapes.
USER 4. The current implementation is correctly handling the case where no red or green cells are adjacent to gray borders.
USER 
USER Next steps:
USER 1. Review and fix the logic for transforming blue plus shapes.
USER 2. Add more detailed debug logging to track the transformation process.
USER 3. Implement additional test cases to verify the transformation of blue plus shapes.
USER 4. Ensure that the transformation is applied to all valid blue plus shapes in the grid.
USER 
USER Unknowns:
USER - Whether there are any edge cases in the grid that are not being handled correctly.
USER - If there are any issues with the is_blue_plus function.
USER 
USER Things to implement:
USER 1. Fix the transformation logic for blue plus shapes.

USER ```
USER from rob_agi.colored_grid import ColoredGrid
USER from typing import List, Tuple, Set
USER import logging
USER import copy
USER 
USER logging.basicConfig(level=logging.DEBUG)
USER logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
USER 
USER def solve_776ffc46(input_grid: ColoredGrid) -> ColoredGrid:
USER     """
USER     Transforms the input grid by changing blue plus shapes to a target color.
USER     The target color (red or green) is determined by the color most frequently
USER     adjacent to gray borders, including diagonally adjacent cells. Only valid
USER     blue plus shapes (5 pixels in a + configuration) are transformed. Other blue
USER     shapes and colors remain unchanged. All transformations are applied simultaneously.
USER 
USER     1. Identify all gray borders in the grid.
USER     2. Count red and green pixels adjacent (including diagonally) to all gray borders collectively.
USER     3. Determine the target color based on the counts (red if tied).
USER     4. Find all blue plus shapes in the grid.
USER     5. Transform these blue plus shapes to the target color.
USER     6. Return the modified grid.
USER 
USER     If no red or green pixels are found adjacent to gray borders, return the original grid.
USER     """
USER     GRAY, BLUE, RED, GREEN = 5, 1, 2, 3
USER     rows, cols = len(input_grid.values), len(input_grid.values[0])
USER 
USER     def is_valid_cell(r: int, c: int) -> bool:
USER         return 0 <= r < rows and 0 <= c < cols
USER 
USER     def get_adjacent_cells(r: int, c: int) -> Set[Tuple[int, int]]:
USER         return {(r+dr, c+dc) for dr in [-1, 0, 1] for dc in [-1, 0, 1] 
USER                 if (dr != 0 or dc != 0) and is_valid_cell(r+dr, c+dc)}
USER 
USER     def is_blue_plus(r: int, c: int) -> bool:
USER         if input_grid.values[r][c] != BLUE:
USER             return False
USER         return all(is_valid_cell(r+dr, c+dc) and input_grid.values[r+dr][c+dc] == BLUE 
USER                    for dr, dc in [(0, 1), (1, 0), (0, -1), (-1, 0)])
USER 
USER     # Step 1 & 2: Identify gray borders and count adjacent colors
USER     border_cells = set()
USER     adjacent_cells = set()
USER     for r in range(rows):
USER         for c in range(cols):
USER             if input_grid.values[r][c] == GRAY:
USER                 border_cells.add((r, c))
USER                 adjacent_cells.update(get_adjacent_cells(r, c))
USER 
USER     adjacent_cells -= border_cells  # Remove gray cells from adjacent cells
USER 
USER     red_count = sum(1 for r, c in adjacent_cells if input_grid.values[r][c] == RED)
USER     green_count = sum(1 for r, c in adjacent_cells if input_grid.values[r][c] == GREEN)
USER 
USER     logger.debug(f"Gray border cells: {border_cells}")
USER     logger.debug(f"Adjacent cells: {adjacent_cells}")
USER     logger.debug(f"Red count: {red_count}, Green count: {green_count}")
USER 
USER     # Step 3: Determine the target color
USER     if red_count == 0 and green_count == 0:
USER         logger.info("No red or green cells adjacent to gray borders. Returning original grid.")
USER         return input_grid
USER 
USER     target_color = RED if red_count >= green_count else GREEN
USER     logger.info(f"Target color: {target_color}")
USER 
USER     # Step 4 & 5: Find and transform blue plus shapes
USER     result_grid = copy.deepcopy(input_grid.values)
USER     for r in range(rows):
USER         for c in range(cols):
USER             if is_blue_plus(r, c):
USER                 for dr, dc in [(0, 0), (0, 1), (1, 0), (0, -1), (-1, 0)]:
USER                     result_grid[r+dr][c+dc] = target_color
USER                 logger.debug(f"Transformed blue plus at ({r}, {c}) to color {target_color}")
USER 
USER     # Step 6: Return the modified grid
USER     return ColoredGrid(values=result_grid)
USER ```

USER Challenge 776ffc46: Transform blue plus shapes in a grid
USER 
USER Key observations:
USER 1. Only blue plus shapes (5 pixels in a + configuration) are transformed.
USER 2. The target color for transformation is determined by the color most frequently adjacent to gray borders.
USER 3. Gray (5) acts as a border and is not part of any region to be transformed.
USER 4. All transformations are applied simultaneously.
USER 5. Diagonal adjacency to gray borders is considered when counting red and green cells.
USER 
USER Rules:
USER 1. Find the target color by counting occurrences of red (2) and green (3) adjacent to all gray borders collectively.
USER 2. Choose the color with the higher count as the target color.
USER 3. If counts are equal, use red as the target color.
USER 4. Identify all blue plus shapes in the grid.
USER 5. Transform these blue plus shapes to the target color.
USER 6. Other blue shapes and colors remain unchanged.
USER 7. If no red or green cells are adjacent to gray borders, return the original grid unchanged.
USER 
USER Current issues:

USER 2. The target color determination is working correctly.
USER 3. The transformation of blue plus shapes is not being applied.

USER Lessons learned:
USER 1. The target color determination is working as expected.
USER 2. Counting occurrences of red and green, including diagonally adjacent cells, is correct.
USER 3. The issue lies in the application of the transformation to blue plus shapes.
USER 4. The current implementation is correctly handling the case where no red or green cells are adjacent to gray borders.

USER A distilled list of common problem-solving techniques for grid-based puzzles:
USER 
USER - Analyze the input grid to identify key features (colors, patterns, shapes).
USER - Create a deep copy of the input grid to avoid modifying the original.
USER - Determine grid dimensions and handle variable-sized inputs.
USER - Identify and count unique colors in the grid.
USER - Detect lines, shapes, or patterns (horizontal, vertical, diagonal).
USER - Find connected regions of the same color.
USER - Identify symmetry or mirroring in the grid.
USER - Extract subgrids or pattern units.
USER - Use flood fill or depth-first search algorithms for region detection.
USER - Apply transformations (rotation, reflection, translation) to grid elements.
USER - Calculate distances or relationships between key elements.
USER - Determine the output grid size based on input characteristics.
USER - Fill new grids with background colors or patterns.
USER - Apply rules or transformations based on relative positions.
USER - Use backtracking for solving constraint satisfaction problems.
USER - Implement color swapping or replacement logic.
USER - Detect and preserve special elements (e.g., fixed points, unique shapes).
USER - Use sorting to order elements or colors based on specific criteria.
USER - Expand or contract grid regions based on rules.
USER - Use mathematical operations (min, max, count) for grid analysis.
USER - Implement pattern matching algorithms for repetitive structures.
USER - Handle edge cases and grid boundaries carefully.
USER - Use modular arithmetic for wrapping patterns or cyclic behaviors.
USER - Apply cellular automata rules for grid evolution.
USER - Implement region growing or shrinking algorithms.```

USER     color_map = {0: '⬛', 1: '🟦', 2: '🟥', 3: '🟩', 4: '🟨', 5: '⬜'}
USER     return '\n'.join(''.join(color_map.get(cell, '❓') for cell in row) for row in grid.values)

USER ```
USER from rob_agi.colored_grid import ColoredGrid
USER from typing import List, Tuple, Set
USER import logging
USER import copy
USER 
USER logging.basicConfig(level=logging.DEBUG)
USER logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
USER 
USER def solve_776ffc46(input_grid: ColoredGrid) -> ColoredGrid:
USER     """
USER     Transforms the input grid by changing blue plus shapes to a target color.
USER     The target color (red or green) is determined by the color most frequently
USER     adjacent to gray borders, including diagonally adjacent cells. Only valid
USER     blue plus shapes (5 pixels in a + configuration) are transformed. Other blue
USER     shapes and colors remain unchanged. All transformations are applied simultaneously.
USER 
USER     1. Identify all gray borders in the grid.
USER     2. Count red and green pixels adjacent (including diagonally) to all gray borders collectively.
USER     3. Determine the target color based on the counts (red if tied).
USER     4. Find all blue plus shapes in the grid.
USER     5. Transform these blue plus shapes to the target color.
USER     6. Return the modified grid.
USER 
USER     If no red or green pixels are found adjacent to gray borders, return the original grid.
USER     """
USER     GRAY, BLUE, RED, GREEN = 5, 1, 2, 3
USER     rows, cols = len(input_grid.values), len(input_grid.values[0])
USER 
USER     def is_valid_cell(r: int, c: int) -> bool:
USER         return 0 <= r < rows and 0 <= c < cols
USER 
USER     def get_adjacent_cells(r: int, c: int) -> Set[Tuple[int, int]]:
USER         return {(r+dr, c+dc) for dr in [-1, 0, 1] for dc in [-1, 0, 1] 
USER                 if (dr != 0 or dc != 0) and is_valid_cell(r+dr, c+dc)}
USER 
USER     def is_blue_plus(r: int, c: int) -> bool:
USER         if input_grid.values[r][c] != BLUE:
USER             return False
USER         return all(is_valid_cell(r+dr, c+dc) and input_grid.values[r+dr][c+dc] == BLUE 
USER                    for dr, dc in [(0, 1), (1, 0), (0, -1), (-1, 0)])
USER 
USER     # Step 1 & 2: Identify gray borders and count adjacent colors
USER     border_cells = set()
USER     adjacent_cells = set()
USER     for r in range(rows):
USER         for c in range(cols):
USER             if input_grid.values[r][c] == GRAY:
USER                 border_cells.add((r, c))
USER                 adjacent_cells.update(get_adjacent_cells(r, c))
USER 
USER     adjacent_cells -= border_cells  # Remove gray cells from adjacent cells
USER 
USER     red_count = sum(1 for r, c in adjacent_cells if input_grid.values[r][c] == RED)
USER     green_count = sum(1 for r, c in adjacent_cells if input_grid.values[r][c] == GREEN)
USER 
USER     logger.debug(f"Gray border cells: {border_cells}")
USER     logger.debug(f"Adjacent cells: {adjacent_cells}")
USER     logger.debug(f"Red count: {red_count}, Green count: {green_count}")
USER 
USER     # Step 3: Determine the target color
USER     if red_count == 0 and green_count == 0:
USER         logger.info("No red or green cells adjacent to gray borders. Returning original grid.")
USER         return input_grid
USER 
USER     target_color = RED if red_count >= green_count else GREEN
USER     logger.info(f"Target color: {target_color}")
USER 
USER     # Step 4 & 5: Find and transform blue plus shapes
USER     result_grid = copy.deepcopy(input_grid.values)
USER     for r in range(rows):
USER         for c in range(cols):
USER             if is_blue_plus(r, c):
USER                 for dr, dc in [(0, 0), (0, 1), (1, 0), (0, -1), (-1, 0)]:
USER                     result_grid[r+dr][c+dc] = target_color
USER                 logger.debug(f"Transformed blue plus at ({r}, {c}) to color {target_color}")
USER 
USER     # Step 6: Return the modified grid
USER     return ColoredGrid(values=result_grid)

USER Challenge 776ffc46: Transform blue plus shapes in a grid
USER 
USER Key observations:
USER 1. Only blue plus shapes (5 pixels in a + configuration) are transformed.
USER 2. The target color for transformation is determined by the color most frequently adjacent to gray borders.
USER 3. Gray (5) acts as a border and is not part of any region to be transformed.
USER 4. All transformations are applied simultaneously.
USER 5. Diagonal adjacency to gray borders is considered when counting red and green cells.
USER 
USER Rules:
USER 1. Find the target color by counting occurrences of red (2) and green (3) adjacent to all gray borders collectively.
USER 2. Choose the color with the higher count as the target color.
USER 3. If counts are equal, use red as the target color.
USER 4. Identify all blue plus shapes in the grid.
USER 5. Transform these blue plus shapes to the target color.
USER 6. Other blue shapes and colors remain unchanged.
USER 7. If no red or green cells are adjacent to gray borders, return the original grid unchanged.
USER 
USER Current issues:
USER 1. The function is correctly identifying red and green cells adjacent to gray borders.
USER 2. The target color determination is working correctly.
USER 3. The transformation of blue plus shapes is not being applied.
USER 
USER Lessons learned:
USER 1. The target color determination is working as expected.
USER 2. Counting occurrences of red and green, including diagonally adjacent cells, is correct.
USER 3. The issue lies in the application of the transformation to blue plus shapes.
USER 4. The current implementation is correctly handling the case where no red or green cells are adjacent to gray borders.
USER 
USER Next steps:
USER 1. Review and fix the logic for transforming blue plus shapes.
USER 2. Add more detailed debug logging to track the transformation process.
USER 3. Implement additional test cases to verify the transformation of blue plus shapes.
USER 4. Ensure that the transformation is applied to all valid blue plus shapes in the grid.
USER 
USER Unknowns:

USER Things to implement:

ASSISTANT Understanding of the challenge:
ASSISTANT The challenge requires transforming blue plus shapes in a grid to either red or green, based on which color is more frequently adjacent to gray borders. The transformation should only apply to valid blue plus shapes (5 pixels in a + configuration), and all transformations should occur simultaneously. Diagonal adjacency to gray borders should be considered when counting red and green cells. If red and green counts are equal, red should be chosen as the target color.
ASSISTANT 
ASSISTANT Proposed solution:
ASSISTANT 1. Identify all gray border cells in the grid.
ASSISTANT 2. Count red and green cells adjacent (including diagonally) to all gray borders collectively.
ASSISTANT 3. Determine the target color based on the counts (red if tied).
ASSISTANT 4. Identify all valid blue plus shapes in the grid, storing their center coordinates.
ASSISTANT 5. Create a new grid as a deep copy of the input grid.
ASSISTANT 6. Transform all identified blue plus shapes to the target color in the new grid.
ASSISTANT 7. Return the transformed grid.
ASSISTANT
